Trigger when the user asks to \"issueize horizontally\", \"horizontal slicing\", \"split by layer\", \"one agent per layer\", \"decompose spec-NNN into layer tasks\", or wants backend and frontend built concurrently against a pre-frozen interface. Unlike the vertical `issueize` skill, the wiring contract between layers is decided up front and handed to every layer agent verbatim, so no layer has to wait for or discover another's output. Writes a component folder per component (contract + one task file per layer) plus an index with the component DAG.\nargument-hint: [spec number or label, e.g. 001 or spec-001-... ; blank = ask / most recent]\nallowed-tools: Read, Write, Bash, Glob, Grep, AskUserQuestion\n---\n\n# Issueize (horizontal)\n\nTake one existing spec and decompose it into **components**, then split each component into **layer tasks** — one per code layer, executable fully in parallel because the interface between them is frozen before any of them starts.\n\nThis is the horizontal counterpart to the `issueize` skill. Use that one when you want complete vertical slices owned end-to-end by a single agent. Use this one when the work divides cleanly along layer boundaries and you want backend, frontend and service layers built concurrently.\n\nThis is decomposition and scoping only — you do **not** implement anything here.\n\n## Core concepts (get these exactly right)\n\n**1. A component is a coarse subcomponent of the task; a layer task is one layer of it.**\nA component is a meaningful chunk of the spec's functionality — broader than a vertical issue, because it is not constrained to what one agent can hold. Within a component, each affected code layer gets exactly one **layer task**: e.g. one backend task (api + service + repo + migration, in `ardoq-api`), one frontend task (`ardoq-packages`), one AI-service task (`devops-monorepo`). All layer tasks of a component are dispatched at the same time and never touch each other's repo.\n\nPrefer **fewer, broader components**. The reason vertical slices must be small is that one agent owns every layer; here the per-agent load is already divided by layer, so a component can carry substantially more functionality before any single task gets unwieldy. Split a component only when its own layer tasks would become too large, or when a genuine dependency forces a later tier.\n\n> \"Layer\" means a **code layer** (api / service / repo / DB / frontend / AI service). \"Tier\" means the A/B/C dependency level of a **component** in the DAG. Never conflate them.\n\n**2. The contract is frozen before the layer tasks are written. This is the whole mechanism.**\nLayer tasks can run in parallel only because neither side has to wait to learn what the other will produce. So for each component you must decide, concretely and completely, every fact that crosses a layer boundary — and then write it once, as an authoritative **contract**, embedded verbatim in every layer task of that component.\n\nA contract is not a sketch. Vague contracts are the single failure mode of horizontal slicing: if the backend invents `workspaceId` while the frontend coded against `workspace_id`, both tasks \"pass\" and the feature is broken. Pin down, exactly:\n- **Endpoints**: full path, HTTP method, path/query params with types, auth/permission expectation, status codes including error cases.\n- **Payloads**: request and response bodies as literal example JSON plus a field table (name, type, required, meaning). Exact field names and casing. State what happens on empty/absent values.\n- **Named things crossing the boundary**: action/event names, feature-flag keys, DB column and table names, queue/topic names, generated-client symbol names, error codes and their meanings.\n- **Ownership**: for every element, which layer task **produces** it and which ones **consume** it.\n- **Versioning/compat** requirements if the element is public or shared.\n\nIf you cannot pin an element down from the spec plus the code, do not paper over it — either resolve it by reading the code, or ask the user. An unresolved contract element is a blocker, not a detail.\n\n**3. Each layer task is defined strictly by its inputs, its outputs, and its exclusions.**\nEvery layer task file states: the contract elements it **consumes** (and from whom), the contract elements it **must produce** (with exact signatures), and the repos/paths it must **not** touch. A layer agent is responsible for its layer only and is explicitly told that the other layers are being built concurrently by other agents against the same contract — so it must code to the contract rather than to what it can currently observe running.\n\n**4. A layer task must be verifiable on its own, against the contract.**\nBecause its counterpart may not exist yet, a layer task's acceptance criteria must be checkable in isolation: the backend task is verified by exercising its endpoints directly (curl/REPL/tests) and matching the contract's payloads; the frontend task is verified against the contract shape (fixtures, mocked client, component tests). Cross-layer integration is verified once, later, by the orchestrator's integration step — not by an individual layer task.\n\n**5. Components form a dependency DAG, tiered for parallelism.**\n- A component with no dependencies is a **root**, tier **A**.\n- Otherwise `tier_index = 1 + max(tier_index of each dependency)` (A=1, B=2, C=3…).\n- Dependencies are between **components**, never between layer tasks of the same component. If you find yourself wanting \"frontend depends on backend\", you have misunderstood this skill: that dependency is exactly what the frozen contract removes.\n- Two components in the same tier must not have conflicting touch-points **within the same repo** — since their same-layer tasks run concurrently in the same repo's worktrees. If they would collide: merge the components, introduce a dependency, or extract the shared surface (shared schema, shared module, migration) into an earlier-tier foundation component.\n\n**6. A real dependency between components is one only the contract cannot dissolve.**\nLegitimate: a later component needs a table, module or shared abstraction that an earlier one physically creates; or the spec's behaviour genuinely layers (feature B operates on data feature A produces). Not legitimate: \"the frontend needs to know the API shape\" — that is contract work, done up front.\n\n## Workflow\n\n### 1. Locate and read the spec\n\nResolve the target spec from the argument (a number like `001`, or a full label). If none given, list `_workflow/specs/spec-*/` and either use the obvious most-recent one or ask which. Read the whole spec — Goals, Non-goals, functional requirements (`FR-N`), acceptance criteria. Every layer task must trace to ≥1 FR; every in-scope FR must be covered.\n\nIf the spec is too vague to pin contracts down, stop and ask. A horizontal decomposition with a loose contract is worse than no decomposition.\n\n### 2. Ground in the real code\n\nGrep/Glob the affected repos (`ardoq-api`, `ardoq-packages`, `devops-monorepo`) to find the actual namespaces, endpoints, tables, components and clients involved, plus a close analogue to mirror for each layer. You cannot write a precise contract from the spec alone — endpoint conventions, serialization casing, auth patterns and generated-client shapes all come from the code. Note per layer: where the code goes, what it must match, and what already exists.\n\n### 3. Cut into components\n\nGroup the spec's functionality into the smallest number of coarse components that respects true dependencies and intra-tier non-collision. Bias toward broad. For each component, list the layers it touches — that determines its layer tasks.\n\nSanity-check each component: *is every cross-layer fact inside it something I can pin down now?* If not, either resolve it or reconsider the boundary.\n\n### 4. Freeze the contract per component\n\nFor each component, write the contract in full (Core Concept 2). Assign each element an owner (producing layer) and its consumers. Then check closure: every element a layer task consumes is produced either by a layer task in the same component, or by an already-completed lower-tier component, or by existing code you have verified exists.\n\n### 5. Derive the layer tasks\n\nFor each component, one task per affected layer. Each is a standalone brief containing the **entire** contract verbatim (not a reference to it — the agent must not have to fetch it), plus that layer's own scope, exclusions, pointers and isolation-testable acceptance criteria.\n\n### 6. Assign tiers and validate\n\nTier the components by the leveling rule, verify acyclicity, run the intra-tier collision check per repo, then run the validation checklist below.\n\n### 7. Write the files and report\n\nWrite the component folders and `index.md` (conventions below). Then report: components per tier, layer tasks per component, the critical path, and any decomposition decisions the user should sanity-check — especially merges, foundation extractions, and any contract element you had to decide yourself rather than read off the spec.\n\n## File & ID conventions\n\nHorizontal output lives in the spec's own folder, under `components/` (so it never collides with a vertical `issues/` decomposition of the same spec):\n\n```\n_workflow/specs/spec-NNN-descriptive-title/\n  spec-NNN-descriptive-title.md\n  components/\n    index.md                                  ← component DAG + tier overview\n    NNN-A1-brief-description/\n      component.md                            ← the frozen contract + shared context\n      NNN-A1-be-brief-description.md          ← backend layer task\n      NNN-A1-fe-brief-description.md          ← frontend layer task\n    NNN-A2-brief-description/\n      component.md\n      NNN-A2-be-brief-description.md\n    NNN-B1-brief-description/\n      ...\n```\n\n**Component ID: `NNN-Xk-brief-description`** — `NNN` spec number (3 digits); `X` tier letter; `k` number within tier; kebab-case slug.\n\n**Layer task ID: `NNN-Xk-<layer>-brief-description`** where `<layer>` is:\n\n| Code | Layer | Repo |\n|---|---|---|\n| `be` | backend (api, service, repo, DB migration) | `ardoq-api` |\n| `fe` | frontend | `ardoq-packages` |\n| `ai` | Python AI services | `devops-monorepo` |\n\nAdd a code only if a new repo genuinely enters scope, and record it in the index. **One layer task per layer per component** — never two tasks in the same repo for the same component (they would collide in the same worktree).\n\nFilenames equal IDs. Example: `004-A1-be-workspace-picker-endpoint.md`.\n\n## Component contract template (`component.md`)\n\n~~~markdown\n# NNN-Xk: <Component Title>\n\n| | |\n|---|---|\n| **Component ID** | NNN-Xk-brief-description |\n| **Spec** | spec-NNN-descriptive-title |\n| **Tier** | X  (parallel with all other tier-X components) |\n| **Depends on** | <component IDs, or `None (root)`> |\n| **Enables** | <component IDs that depend on this> |\n| **Satisfies** | FR-<n>, FR-<m> |\n| **Layer tasks** | NNN-Xk-be-…, NNN-Xk-fe-… (dispatched concurrently) |\n\n## Objective\n\nOne or two sentences: the functionality this component delivers once all its layer tasks are done.\n\n## FROZEN CONTRACT\n\nAuthoritative. Every layer task implements exactly this. No layer may change, rename, extend or \"improve\" any element here; a mismatch found later is resolved by fixing the deviating layer, not by renegotiating the contract. If an element turns out to be genuinely unimplementable, the layer agent stops and escalates rather than improvising a different shape.\n\n### Endpoints\n\n#### `<METHOD> /api/<path>`\n- **Produced by:** NNN-Xk-be-… · **Consumed by:** NNN-Xk-fe-…\n- **Auth / permission:** <e.g. `:allowed?` requires write on workspace>\n- **Path / query params:** <name — type — required? — meaning>\n- **Request body:**\n```json\n{ \"name\": \"string\", \"workspaceId\": \"string\" }\n```\n- **Response 200:**\n```json\n{ \"_id\": \"string\", \"name\": \"string\", \"created\": \"ISO-8601 string\" }\n```\n- **Errors:** `400` <when / body shape>; `403` <when>; `404` <when>\n\n### Field tables\n\n| Field | Type | Required | Meaning | Notes |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `workspaceId` | string | yes | target workspace | exact casing as written; camelCase on the wire |\n\n### Other boundary-crossing names\n\n| Element | Kind | Exact name | Produced by | Consumed by |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| … | action / flag / column / topic / client symbol | `…` | NNN-Xk-be-… | NNN-Xk-fe-… |\n\n### Empty / edge-case behaviour\n\n- <e.g. absent `foo` ⇒ `null` in the response, not omitted>\n- <e.g. empty list ⇒ `[]` plus `200`, never `404`>\n\n## Shared context\n\nFacts every layer task needs: the spec sections in play, the analogue features to mirror per layer, relevant conventions and gotchas.\n\n## Component acceptance criteria (integration-level)\n\nChecked by the orchestrator's integration step once all layer tasks are merged — not by any single layer task.\n- [ ] <observable end-to-end behaviour, traceable to an FR>\n\n## Layer task summary\n\n| Layer task | Repo | Produces | Consumes |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| NNN-Xk-be-… | ardoq-api | `POST /api/foo` | `foo` table (NNN-A1) |\n| NNN-Xk-fe-… | ardoq-packages | `FooForm` | `POST /api/foo` |\n~~~\n\n## Layer task template\n\nEach layer task is a **standalone brief** — one layer agent executes it with only this file and its repo. It contains the full contract, copied verbatim from `component.md`.\n\n~~~markdown\n# NNN-Xk-<layer>: <Layer Task Title>\n\n| | |\n|---|---|\n| **Task ID** | NNN-Xk-<layer>-brief-description |\n| **Component** | NNN-Xk-brief-description |\n| **Spec** | spec-NNN-descriptive-title |\n| **Layer** | <backend / frontend / AI service> |\n| **Repo** | <ardoq-api / ardoq-packages / devops-monorepo> |\n| **Tier** | X |\n| **Runs in parallel with** | <sibling layer task IDs in this component> |\n| **Satisfies** | FR-<n> |\n\n## Objective\n\nOne or two sentences: what this layer must deliver for the component to work.\n\n## Parallel-work notice\n\nThe other layers of this component are being built **right now** by other agents against the identical contract below. Consequences:\n- Code to the **contract**, not to what you can currently observe running. A counterpart endpoint/component may not exist yet; that is expected and is not a blocker.\n- Do **not** implement, stub into the repo, or \"temporarily\" add anything that belongs to another layer.\n- Do **not** alter any contract element. If one is genuinely unimplementable, stop and escalate — do not substitute a different shape.\n- Stay inside your own repo. Touching another layer's repo will collide with a concurrently running agent.\n\n## FROZEN CONTRACT\n\n<verbatim copy of the component's FROZEN CONTRACT section>\n\n## Inputs — what you receive\n\n- **From the contract:** <elements this layer consumes, with exact names>\n- **From lower-tier components:** <e.g. the `foo` table created by NNN-A1-be-…>\n- **From existing code:** <verified existing helpers/endpoints/components to build on, with paths>\n\n## Outputs — what you must produce\n\nExact, contract-matching deliverables. Another agent is coding against these names right now.\n- <e.g. `POST /api/foo` handler with `:allowed?`, returning the Response 200 body exactly as specified>\n- <e.g. `foo-service/create!` — signature: …>\n\n## Scope — in\n\nChecklist for this layer only.\n- [ ] <…>\n- [ ] <tests proving this layer satisfies the contract in isolation>\n\n## Scope — out (do NOT touch)\n\n- <the sibling layers' repos, named explicitly>\n- <files owned by other same-tier components in this repo>\n\n## Context & pointers\n\n- Analogue to mirror: <path to a similar existing implementation in this repo>\n- <conventions, constraints, gotchas>\n\n## Acceptance criteria (verifiable without the other layers)\n\n- [ ] <e.g. `curl POST /api/foo` with the contract request returns the contract 200 body, field-for-field>\n- [ ] <e.g. `FooForm` renders and submits the contract request shape against a mocked client>\n\n## Definition of done\n\n- [ ] Every in-scope item implemented in this repo only\n- [ ] Every contract element this layer produces matches the contract exactly — names, casing, types, status codes\n- [ ] Tests written and passing\n- [ ] Nothing belonging to another layer added\n- [ ] Conforms to repo conventions\n~~~\n\n## Index template (`components/index.md`)\n\n~~~markdown\n# spec-NNN — Component graph (horizontal)\n\nGenerated from spec-NNN-descriptive-title. You do not write feature code — you dispatch specialist agents, guard the frozen contracts, manage git worktrees and merges, and gate the result. Track every step with TodoWrite.\n\nThis is the horizontal counterpart to the `orchestrate` skill. There, one augmentor owns a vertical slice across all layers. Here, each **component** is built by **one augmentor per code layer, all at once**, and the thing that makes that possible is the component's **frozen contract**. Your central job, beyond git, is being the contract's custodian: every layer agent gets it verbatim, no layer agent gets to change it, and you verify conformance before you merge.\n\nIf the spec has an `issues/` folder rather than a `components/` folder, it was decomposed vertically — use the `orchestrate` skill instead, or ask the user.\n\n## Inputs\n\nCollect these before starting; ask (AskUserQuestion) for any that are missing:\n\n1. **Repos to work in** — one or more of `ardoq-api`, `ardoq-packages`, `devops-monorepo`.\n2. **Spec** — the `_workflow/specs/spec-NNN-.../spec-NNN-....md` file (source of truth for correctness).\n3. **Components** — that spec's `components/` folder (`index.md`, plus one folder per component containing `component.md` and the layer task files).\n4. **Base branch(es)** — the integration branch **per repo**, assigned by the user. Names may differ between repos (e.g. `ardoq-api` on `master`, `devops-monorepo` on `main`). Record every one. In each repo, worktrees branch from that repo's base branch and merge back into that same branch.\n5. **Run testers?** — optional per-layer-task functional testing. If the user hasn't said, ask (yes/no). The cross-layer integration check (below) is **not** optional and runs regardless.\n\n## Agent map\n\n| Situation | Agent | Model |\n|---|---|---|\n| Implement one layer of a component | `augmentor` | latest Sonnet |\n| Convention review of changes in `ardoq-api` | `ardoq-backend-enforcer` | latest Sonnet |\n| Convention review of changes in `ardoq-packages` | `ardoq-frontend-enforcer` | latest Sonnet |\n| Convention review of changes in `devops-monorepo` | `ardoq-ai-enforcer` | latest Sonnet |\n| Functional test of one layer task against the contract | `tester` | latest Sonnet |\n| Cross-layer integration check of a finished component | `tester` | latest Sonnet |\n| Fix a bug or a contract deviation | `bugger` | latest Sonnet |\n| Fill missing/incomplete functionality | `augmentor` | latest Sonnet |\n| Final whole-spec quality gate | `qa` | latest Opus |\n\nPass `model` explicitly on every Agent call.\n\n## Preflight\n\n1. Read the spec and `components/index.md`. Derive the **tiers** (A, B, C…) and the component DAG. Confirm the ordering.\n2. For each component, read `component.md` and **every** layer task file. Note which repos the component touches and which layer task owns each.\n3. **Audit the contracts before dispatching anything.** For each component, check that its contract is actually frozen and closed: every endpoint has method, path, params, request and response bodies, status codes and error cases; every wire field has a literal name with exact casing; every boundary-crossing name is a literal, not a description; every element a task consumes is produced by a sibling task, a lower-tier component, or existing code. Check the contract copy in each layer task matches `component.md` verbatim. **If a contract is vague, internally inconsistent, or differs between task files, halt and report it** — dispatching layer agents against a loose contract produces two \"passing\" layers that don't fit together, which is the most expensive failure this pipeline can produce.\n4. In each repo in scope: verify that repo's base branch exists, the working tree is clean, and it's up to date. Abort and report if any repo is dirty or its base branch is missing.\n5. **Record your ownership manifest** (see \"Branch ownership and merge verification\"): each repo's base branch name and current tip SHA. List any pre-existing `.orchestrate/` worktrees and `comp/*` / `qa/*` branches you did **not** create — they belong to another orchestrator, are off-limits, and are never cleaned up by you.\n6. Build the plan in TodoWrite: one tracked item per layer task, one per component integration check, plus the QA gate.\n\n## Git worktree model\n\nAll git operations are **per repo** (separate repositories, each with its own base branch). For a component touching N repos, create **N worktrees** — one in each affected repo, branched from that repo's base branch. **Each layer agent gets exactly one worktree, and no worktree is shared by two agents.** That is the structural advantage here: layer isolation is enforced by the filesystem.\n\nPer component, per affected repo (`<base>` = that repo's base branch):\n```bash\ngit -C <repo> worktree add ../.orchestrate/<component-id>/<repo> -b comp/<component-id> <base>\n```\n- The layer augmentor, that repo's enforcer, and any fix agents work **only** inside that worktree path — always pass the absolute path and tell them to operate there. Do **not** use the Agent tool's built-in `isolation: worktree`; you need durable, named branches you merge yourself.\n- **One commit per component per repo**, authored by you once that repo's layer work (augment + enforce + any test-driven fixes) is complete and passing. Message references the component ID, e.g. `004-A1 <component title>`.\n- Merge the component branch into the base branch on the main worktree, then remove the worktree. **Never merge without the guard below.**\n```bash\n# GUARD: the shared checkout may have been switched by someone else since you last looked.\ntest \"$(git -C <repo> branch --show-current)\" = \"<base>\" || { echo \"HALT: not on <base>\"; exit 1; }\ntest -z \"$(git -C <repo> status --porcelain)\" || { echo \"HALT: dirty\"; exit 1; }\nPRE=$(git -C <repo> rev-parse HEAD)\n\ngit -C <repo> merge --no-ff comp/<component-id> -m \"Merge <component-id>\"\n\n# VERIFY: landed on the intended branch, on top of the intended commit.\ntest \"$(git -C <repo> branch --show-current)\" = \"<base>\" || echo \"PROBLEM: merged elsewhere\"\ntest \"$(git -C <repo> rev-parse HEAD^1)\" = \"$PRE\" || echo \"PROBLEM: unexpected first parent\"\ngit -C <repo> merge-base --is-ancestor comp/<component-id> <base> || echo \"PROBLEM: component not in base\"\n\ngit -C <repo> worktree remove ../.orchestrate/<component-id>/<repo>\ngit -C <repo> branch -d comp/<component-id>\n```\n\nBecause tier N+1 depends on tier N, at the end of each tier **every worktree from that tier must be committed and merged into its repo's base branch — across all repos — and every component in the tier must have passed its integration check, before any tier N+1 worktree is created.**\n\n## Branch ownership and merge verification\n\n**Assume you are not the only orchestrator running.** Other specs may be in flight in the same clones, driven by other sessions. The main checkout of each repo is *shared mutable state*: its `HEAD` can be switched to a different branch between any two of your commands, by someone you cannot see. A merge issued while the shared checkout sits on a foreign branch silently lands your work on that branch and contaminates someone else's spec. This is one of the worst failures this pipeline can produce, and it is invisible unless you check for it.\n\n**Know what is yours.** At preflight, write down explicitly — and keep updated:\n- the base branch **name and tip SHA** for each repo in scope, as assigned by the user;\n- the `comp/<component-id>` and `qa/spec-NNN` branches **you** created;\n- the worktree paths **you** created under `.orchestrate/`.\n\nEverything else in every repo — other branches, other `.orchestrate/` worktrees, other specs' commits, the shared checkout's choice of branch — is **foreign**. Treat it as read-only, always.\n\n**Verify every merge, both sides.** Never trust an earlier reading of the current branch; re-check immediately before each merge. For every merge (component merges and the QA merge alike):\n\n1. **Before:** assert the shared checkout is on the branch you intend to merge *into*, and its tree is clean. Record the pre-merge tip SHA.\n2. **Before:** assert the branch you are merging *from* is one you created, and is based on the intended base (`git merge-base --is-ancestor <base> <source>`).\n3. **After:** assert the merge landed on the intended branch, the new tip's first parent is the recorded pre-merge tip, and the source is now an ancestor of the base.\n4. **After the last merge in a repo:** assert the base branch contains every commit you made in that repo, and that no branch you don't own has gained any of them.\n\nIf any assertion fails, **halt** — do not improvise a repair. Report exactly what landed where.\n\n**Never `git checkout` a branch you don't own.** If the shared checkout is on a foreign branch when you need to merge, do not switch it: halt and ask the user.\n\n## Contract custody\n\nThe contract is the load-bearing element of this pipeline. Your obligations:\n\n- **Hand it over verbatim.** Every layer augmentor receives the full contract text in its prompt — not a file path, not a summary. Also tell it, explicitly: the sibling layers are being built concurrently by other agents against this identical contract; code to the contract, not to what is currently observable; do not implement or stub anything belonging to another layer; do not touch another layer's repo; do not alter any contract element.\n- **The contract outranks any layer's judgement.** When a layer agent reports that an element is awkward, misnamed, or would be better shaped differently, the answer is no. It implements the contract as written.\n- **Verify conformance before merging.** After the augmentor and enforcer finish in a repo, check that repo's diff against the contract yourself: exact paths, methods, field names and casing, types, status codes, error bodies, and the literal names of actions/flags/columns. A near-miss on casing is a total failure of the seam, and it is cheap to catch here and expensive to catch later. If the layer deviated, dispatch a fix agent to bring it to the contract — never adjust the contract to match what the agent happened to write.\n- **Amending a contract is your decision, and it is loud.** If an element is genuinely unimplementable (the platform can't express it, or it contradicts something real in the code), then: halt work on that component, decide the minimal corrected element, update `component.md` **and** the embedded copy in every affected layer task file, record the amendment and its reason in the component file, re-dispatch **every** affected layer (including ones that already \"finished\" against the old element), and note it for the final report. Never let two layers hold different versions of the contract.\n- **Never amend a contract to resolve a merge conflict or a failing test.** Those are implementation problems.\n\n## Main loop — tier by tier, component by component, layer by layer\n\nProcess tiers in order (A, then B, …). Within a tier, components are independent and non-colliding (guaranteed by `issueize-horizontal`), so components may run concurrently; serialize the merges and treat the tier as a barrier.\n\nFor **each component** in the current tier:\n\n1. **Create the worktree(s)** — one per repo the component touches (see model above).\n2. **Dispatch all of the component's layer augmentors concurrently** — one Agent call per layer task, all in a single message so they actually run in parallel. Each gets:\n   - its own layer task file, read in full;\n   - the component's **frozen contract, verbatim**;\n   - the relevant spec context;\n   - the absolute path of **its own repo's worktree only**, with instruction to work exclusively there;\n   - the parallel-work rules (contract custody, above);\n   - the **no-planning-vocabulary-in-the-codebase** rule (below);\n   - instruction **not to commit** — you own the commit.\n   - Reuse a previous augmentor via `SendMessage` when the next layer task in the **same repo and same layer** plainly continues what that agent just finished (extends the same endpoint, module or component) — it keeps the context warm and costs cached tokens instead of a fresh exploration pass. Send it the new task file and the new worktree path explicitly, and tell it the previous worktree is gone; do not assume it remembers the brief. Do not reuse across layers or repos, and do not reuse an agent whose previous run went badly.\n3. **Convention pass per repo** — once a repo's layer augmentor is done, dispatch that repo's enforcer (see Agent map) with the worktree path and the scope (`git -C <worktree> diff <base>`). It minimally fixes convention deviations in place; note anything it reports rather than fixes. Tell it to also strip any planning vocabulary that leaked into the code. Repos are independent here: enforce each as soon as its layer finishes rather than waiting for the slowest layer.\n4. **Contract conformance check per layer** — you do this yourself, per repo, before that repo's commit (see \"Contract custody\"). Fix deviations via a fix agent in that worktree; re-run the scoped enforcer if code changed.\n5. **Optional per-layer tester loop** (only if testers are enabled) — one `tester` per layer task, pointed at that repo's worktree, given **(1)** what the layer must deliver (the task objective and its Outputs) and **(2)** what working looks like (the task's isolation-testable acceptance criteria plus the contract). Tell it explicitly that sibling layers may not exist yet, so it must verify against the contract in isolation and must not treat a missing counterpart as a failure. On failure, dispatch the appropriate fix agent (`bugger` for a bug, `augmentor` for missing functionality) in that worktree, re-run the scoped enforcer if code changed, then re-run the tester. Repeat until it passes; if the loop stops making progress, **stop and escalate**.\n6. **Commit** — one commit per affected repo, referencing the component ID.\n7. **Merge** — merge each repo's component branch into that repo's base branch, once per affected repo, then remove each worktree.\n8. **Cross-layer integration check — mandatory, after all of the component's layers are merged.** This is the step that replaces the wiring an individual vertical slice would have done, and it is the only point at which the seam is actually exercised. Dispatch a `tester` against the merged base branches of every repo the component touches, with: the component's objective, its **integration-level acceptance criteria**, and the full contract. Ask it to exercise the real path across layers — frontend calling the actual backend endpoint, the real payloads, the real names — and to report any mismatch at the boundary specifically (field name, casing, type, status code, missing element) rather than only a generic failure.\n   - **On mismatch:** identify which side deviated from the contract and fix **that** side. The contract is authoritative; do not \"meet in the middle\". Fixes at this point happen on a fresh worktree off the base branch (`git -C <repo> worktree add ../.orchestrate/<component-id>-fix/<repo> -b comp/<component-id>-fix <base>`), get their own commit and merge with the same guards, and are followed by re-running the integration check.\n   - If both sides match the contract and the feature still doesn't work end-to-end, the **contract** is wrong: follow the amendment procedure in \"Contract custody\".\n   - If the integration check won't converge, **stop and escalate**.\n\nComplete every component in the tier through step 8 before creating any worktree for the next tier.\n\n## QA gate (after all tiers are merged and integration-checked)\n\n1. Create a **final worktree** off each repo's (now fully-merged) base branch, one per repo in scope:\n   `git -C <repo> worktree add ../.orchestrate/qa/<repo> -b qa/spec-NNN <base>`.\n2. Dispatch the **`qa`** agent (latest Opus) with the spec, the components (contracts included), the repos, and the QA worktree path(s). Tell it explicitly that the work was built horizontally, so the risks concentrated at the **layer seams**: contract conformance across repos, and functionality that fell between two layers because neither task claimed it. QA reviews the sum total, writes/runs real unit tests, and orchestrates its own remediation as needed.\n3. **If QA escalates** (fundamental failure — doesn't work, or what was built isn't what the spec asked for): **do not merge.** Stop and report QA's escalation (spec-required vs delivered, the gap).\n4. **If QA signs off**: commit QA's changes as a **final commit** on the QA worktree of each repo it touched, merge each repo's QA branch into that repo's base branch, and remove the worktrees.\n\n## Strip the AI markers — last step, and leave it uncommitted\n\nAugmentors mark every production function they write or edit with `AI-GENERATED: REVIEW` (host comment syntax: Clojure `;; …`, TS/JS `// …`, Python `# …`). Those markers are committed throughout the pipeline. **After the QA merge and before you report, remove every one of them — in place on each repo's checked-out base branch — and do NOT commit the removal.**\n\nThe leftover working-tree diff is the deliverable: each removed marker line sits directly above a function an agent authored, so `git diff` becomes a precise index of agent-written code for the user to review. Committing it, or stripping the markers earlier, destroys that signal.\n\n- **You do this yourself.** Do not delegate it — a subagent will drift into \"tidying\" adjacent code, and this must be a pure comment-line deletion.\n- Do it **in the main checkout** of each repo, on the base branch the user assigned — not in a worktree (they're removed by now).\n- Delete **whole lines** that are solely the marker comment. Never touch a line carrying code, and never remove the surrounding docstring or comment.\n- Sweep **every repo in scope** — with one agent per layer, markers are spread across more repos than in the vertical pipeline. Markers land in test scopes only by mistake; if you find one under `test/`, delete it too.\n- **Verify before reporting**: grep each repo for `AI-GENERATED` — expect zero hits — and confirm `git diff` contains *only* marker-line deletions. If the diff shows any other change, stop and tell the user rather than committing over it.\n- Leave every repo with a **clean index and a dirty working tree**, and say so explicitly in your report.\n\n## Final report to the user\n\nOnce QA has signed off and the QA worktree is merged (or once you've stopped on an escalation/failure), report **tersely**:\n\n- **Per component** (one line each): `NNN-Xk <title> — layers built (be/fe/ai) — integration check result — merged`.\n- **Per layer task** only where something notable happened (a fix loop, a contract deviation, an enforcer finding).\n- **Contract events**: every conformance deviation you caught and which side was fixed; every contract amendment, what changed and why, and which layers were re-dispatched. State plainly if there were none.\n- **Overall**: what was built, and which pipeline steps ran (parallel layer augment → enforce → conformance check → [layer test] → merge → integration check → QA).\n- **Notable details**: convention issues enforcers only reported, merge conflicts, QA-added tests, escalations.\n- **The marker diff**: state that the `AI-GENERATED: REVIEW` markers have been stripped and left **uncommitted**, and that the resulting `git diff` marks every agent-authored function for review.\n\nDo not pad the report. Concise and factual.\n\n## Stop conditions (report and halt — don't spin)\n\n- A component's contract is vague, inconsistent, or differs between its layer task files (preflight audit).\n- A repo is dirty or the base branch is missing at preflight.\n- The shared checkout of a repo is on a branch you don't own when you need to merge.\n- Any pre- or post-merge assertion fails, or work of yours is found on a branch you don't own.\n- A layer augmentor cannot complete its task, or a non-trivial merge conflict arises.\n- A layer tester or the integration check won't converge.\n- A contract element is genuinely unimplementable and the amendment is not obviously minimal — ask rather than redesign the seam yourself.\n- QA escalates a fundamental failure.\n\nIn every stop case, leave the base branches in a known state, report exactly where the pipeline halted and why, and do not sign off.\n\n## Rules\n\n- **Respect the DAG**: tiers strictly in order; a tier is a barrier; later worktrees branch from a base containing all prior tiers and their integration fixes.\n- **Layers run in parallel, always.** Never serialize a component's layers \"to be safe\" — if you feel the need to, the contract is inadequate, which is a halt, not a workaround.\n- **One agent per worktree, one worktree per repo per component.** Never put two agents in the same worktree.\n- **The contract is frozen.** You are its custodian: hand it over verbatim, verify conformance before merging, and amend it only by the loud procedure above.\n- **The integration check is mandatory** for every component, and runs on the merged base branches, not in a worktree.\n- **One commit per component per repo** (plus fix commits and one final QA commit). The final marker strip is the one change you deliberately leave **uncommitted**.\n- **Enforcers are scoped** to the specific component's changes in the specific repo — never a whole-repo sweep.\n- **You delegate all code changes** — you do not write feature code, fixes, or tests yourself; you sequence the agents, guard the contracts, and own git.\n- **No planning vocabulary in the codebase.** The spec/component apparatus lives in `_workflow/` and is invisible to everyone else. Nothing shipped into a repo may reference it: no `FR-3`, no `spec-004`, no `004-A1-be`, no `_workflow/...` paths, no \"as required by the spec\" / \"per the contract\". This covers code comments, docstrings, test and `testing` description strings, commit messages, and any markdown committed to the repo. Pass this rule verbatim to every augmentor, and have each enforcer strip any that slipped through.\n  - Say what the code *does*, not which requirement asked for it: `(testing \"51 rows back means 50 returned and a further page announced\")`, not `(testing \"... announced (FR-7, FR-8)\")`.\n  - Real, shared, resolvable tickets are fine and wanted — Jira keys like `INT26-16` or `ARD-1234` stay.\n  - Traceability belongs in the component/task files (which cite the code), not in the code.\n- **Verify every merge before and after** — re-read the current branch immediately before merging; never rely on an earlier reading.\n- **Stay inside your own area of responsibility — absolutely, no exceptions.** Under no circumstances change anything belonging to another orchestrator: no `checkout`, `reset`, `branch -f`, `rebase`, `commit`, `merge`, branch deletion, worktree removal, or file edit on any branch, worktree, or commit you did not create, and none on a base branch other than the ones the user assigned you. This holds **even when repairing your own mistake.** If you discover you have contaminated a foreign branch, do not undo it: halt, and report precisely what you did, which branch and commits are affected, the branch's pre-contamination tip SHA, and the exact commands that would restore it. Whether to run them is the user's call.\n- **Never sign off yourself** — sign-off is QA's; you report QA's decision.\nSKILLEOF\necho written"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":9007,"cache_read_input_tokens":41374,"output_tokens":8734,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":235},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":9007},"inference_geo":"global","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":8734,"cache_read_input_tokens":41374,"cache_creation_input_tokens":9007,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":9007,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"wireToolInputs":{"toolu_01Bdez5xtxuhRS975Um64Tcf":{"command":"mkdir -p ~/Documents/git/repos/ardoq/.claude/skills/orchestrate-horizontal && cat > ~/Documents/git/repos/ardoq/.claude/skills/orchestrate-horizontal/SKILL.md <<'SKILLEOF'\n---\nname: orchestrate-horizontal\nversion: 1.0.0\ndescription: Turn this Claude instance into the pipeline orchestrator for a horizontally-sliced spec — one produced by the `issueize-horizontal` skill, with a `components/` folder of frozen contracts and per-layer tasks. For a component touching N repos, create **N worktrees** — one in each affected repo, branched from that repo's base branch. **Each layer agent gets exactly one worktree, and no worktree is shared by two agents.** That is the structural advantage here: layer isolation is enforced by the filesystem.\n\nPer component, per affected repo (`<base>` = that repo's base branch):\n```bash\ngit -C <repo> worktree add ../.orchestrate/<component-id>/<repo> -b comp/<component-id> <base>\n```\n- The layer augmentor, that repo's enforcer, and any fix agents work **only** inside that worktree path — always pass the absolute path and tell them to operate there. Do **not** use the Agent tool's built-in `isolation: worktree`; you need durable, named branches you merge yourself.\n- **One commit per component per repo**, authored by you once that repo's layer work (augment + enforce + any test-driven fixes) is complete and passing. Message references the component ID, e.g. `004-A1 <component title>`.\n- Merge the component branch into the base branch on the main worktree, then remove the worktree. **Never merge without the guard below.**\n```bash\n# GUARD: the shared checkout may have been switched by someone else since you last looked.\ntest \"$(git -C <repo> branch --show-current)\" = \"<base>\" || { echo \"HALT: not on <base>\"; exit 1; }\ntest -z \"$(git -C <repo> status --porcelain)\" || { echo \"HALT: dirty\"; exit 1; }\nPRE=$(git -C <repo> rev-parse HEAD)\n\ngit -C <repo> merge --no-ff comp/<component-id> -m \"Merge <component-id>\"\n\n# VERIFY: landed on the intended branch, on top of the intended commit.\ntest \"$(git -C <repo> branch --show-current)\" = \"<base>\" || echo \"PROBLEM: merged elsewhere\"\ntest \"$(git -C <repo> rev-parse HEAD^1)\" = \"$PRE\" || echo \"PROBLEM: unexpected first parent\"\ngit -C <repo> merge-base --is-ancestor comp/<component-id> <base> || echo \"PROBLEM: component not in base\"\n\ngit -C <repo> worktree remove ../.orchestrate/<component-id>/<repo>\ngit -C <repo> branch -d comp/<component-id>\n```\n\nBecause tier N+1 depends on tier N, at the end of each tier **every worktree from that tier must be committed and merged into its repo's base branch — across all repos — and every component in the tier must have passed its integration check, before any tier N+1 worktree is created.**\n\n## Branch ownership and merge verification\n\n**Assume you are not the only orchestrator running.** Other specs may be in flight in the same clones, driven by other sessions. The main checkout of each repo is *shared mutable state*: its `HEAD` can be switched to a different branch between any two of your commands, by someone you cannot see. A merge issued while the shared checkout sits on a foreign branch silently lands your work on that branch and contaminates someone else's spec. This is one of the worst failures this pipeline can produce, and it is invisible unless you check for it.\n\n**Know what is yours.** At preflight, write down explicitly — and keep updated:\n- the base branch **name and tip SHA** for each repo in scope, as assigned by the user;\n- the `comp/<component-id>` and `qa/spec-NNN` branches **you** created;\n- the worktree paths **you** created under `.orchestrate/`.\n\nEverything else in every repo — other branches, other `.orchestrate/` worktrees, other specs' commits, the shared checkout's choice of branch — is **foreign**. Treat it as read-only, always.\n\n**Verify every merge, both sides.** Never trust an earlier reading of the current branch; re-check immediately before each merge. For every merge (component merges and the QA merge alike):\n\n1. **Before:** assert the shared checkout is on the branch you intend to merge *into*, and its tree is clean. Record the pre-merge tip SHA.\n2. **Before:** assert the branch you are merging *from* is one you created, and is based on the intended base (`git merge-base --is-ancestor <base> <source>`).\n3. **After:** assert the merge landed on the intended branch, the new tip's first parent is the recorded pre-merge tip, and the source is now an ancestor of the base.\n4. **After the last merge in a repo:** assert the base branch contains every commit you made in that repo, and that no branch you don't own has gained any of them.\n\nIf any assertion fails, **halt** — do not improvise a repair. Report exactly what landed where.\n\n**Never `git checkout` a branch you don't own.** If the shared checkout is on a foreign branch when you need to merge, do not switch it: halt and ask the user.\n\n## Contract custody\n\nThe contract is the load-bearing element of this pipeline. Your obligations:\n\n- **Hand it over verbatim.** Every layer augmentor receives the full contract text in its prompt — not a file path, not a summary. Also tell it, explicitly: the sibling layers are being built concurrently by other agents against this identical contract; code to the contract, not to what is currently observable; do not implement or stub anything belonging to another layer; do not touch another layer's repo; do not alter any contract element.\n- **The contract outranks any layer's judgement.** When a layer agent reports that an element is awkward, misnamed, or would be better shaped differently, the answer is no. It implements the contract as written.\n- **Verify conformance before merging.** After the augmentor and enforcer finish in a repo, check that repo's diff against the contract yourself: exact paths, methods, field names and casing, types, status codes, error bodies, and the literal names of actions/flags/columns. A near-miss on casing is a total failure of the seam, and it is cheap to catch here and expensive to catch later. If the layer deviated, dispatch a fix agent to bring it to the contract — never adjust the contract to match what the agent happened to write.\n- **Amending a contract is your decision, and it is loud.** If an element is genuinely unimplementable (the platform can't express it, or it contradicts something real in the code), then: halt work on that component, decide the minimal corrected element, update `component.md` **and** the embedded copy in every affected layer task file, record the amendment and its reason in the component file, re-dispatch **every** affected layer (including ones that already \"finished\" against the old element), and note it for the final report. Never let two layers hold different versions of the contract.\n- **Never amend a contract to resolve a merge conflict or a failing test.** Those are implementation problems.\n\n## Main loop — tier by tier, component by component, layer by layer\n\nProcess tiers in order (A, then B, …). Within a tier, components are independent and non-colliding (guaranteed by `issueize-horizontal`), so components may run concurrently; serialize the merges and treat the tier as a barrier.\n\nFor **each component** in the current tier:\n\n1. **Create the worktree(s)** — one per repo the component touches (see model above).\n2. **Dispatch all of the component's layer augmentors concurrently** — one Agent call per layer task, all in a single message so they actually run in parallel. Each gets:\n   - its own layer task file, read in full;\n   - the component's **frozen contract, verbatim**;\n   - the relevant spec context;\n   - the absolute path of **its own repo's worktree only**, with instruction to work exclusively there;\n   - the parallel-work rules (contract custody, above);\n   - the **no-planning-vocabulary-in-the-codebase** rule (below);\n   - instruction **not to commit** — you own the commit.\n   - Reuse a previous augmentor via `SendMessage` when the next layer task in the **same repo and same layer** plainly continues what that agent just finished (extends the same endpoint, module or component) — it keeps the context warm and costs cached tokens instead of a fresh exploration pass. Send it the new task file and the new worktree path explicitly, and tell it the previous worktree is gone; do not assume it remembers the brief. Do not reuse across layers or repos, and do not reuse an agent whose previous run went badly.\n3. **Convention pass per repo** — once a repo's layer augmentor is done, dispatch that repo's enforcer (see Agent map) with the worktree path and the scope (`git -C <worktree> diff <base>`). It minimally fixes convention deviations in place; note anything it reports rather than fixes. Tell it to also strip any planning vocabulary that leaked into the code. Repos are independent here: enforce each as soon as its layer finishes rather than waiting for the slowest layer.\n4. **Contract conformance check per layer** — you do this yourself, per repo, before that repo's commit (see \"Contract custody\"). Fix deviations via a fix agent in that worktree; re-run the scoped enforcer if code changed.\n5. **Optional per-layer tester loop** (only if testers are enabled) — one `tester` per layer task, pointed at that repo's worktree, given **(1)** what the layer must deliver (the task objective and its Outputs) and **(2)** what working looks like (the task's isolation-testable acceptance criteria plus the contract). Tell it explicitly that sibling layers may not exist yet, so it must verify against the contract in isolation and must not treat a missing counterpart as a failure. On failure, dispatch the appropriate fix agent (`bugger` for a bug, `augmentor` for missing functionality) in that worktree, re-run the scoped enforcer if code changed, then re-run the tester. Repeat until it passes; if the loop stops making progress, **stop and escalate**.\n6. **Commit** — one commit per affected repo, referencing the component ID.\n7. **Merge** — merge each repo's component branch into that repo's base branch, once per affected repo, then remove each worktree.\n8. **Cross-layer integration check — mandatory, after all of the component's layers are merged.** This is the step that replaces the wiring an individual vertical slice would have done, and it is the only point at which the seam is actually exercised. Dispatch a `tester` against the merged base branches of every repo the component touches, with: the component's objective, its **integration-level acceptance criteria**, and the full contract. Ask it to exercise the real path across layers — frontend calling the actual backend endpoint, the real payloads, the real names — and to report any mismatch at the boundary specifically (field name, casing, type, status code, missing element) rather than only a generic failure.\n   - **On mismatch:** identify which side deviated from the contract and fix **that** side. The contract is authoritative; do not \"meet in the middle\". Fixes at this point happen on a fresh worktree off the base branch (`git -C <repo> worktree add ../.orchestrate/<component-id>-fix/<repo> -b comp/<component-id>-fix <base>`), get their own commit and merge with the same guards, and are followed by re-running the integration check.\n   - If both sides match the contract and the feature still doesn't work end-to-end, the **contract** is wrong: follow the amendment procedure in \"Contract custody\".\n   - If the integration check won't converge, **stop and escalate**.\n\nComplete every component in the tier through step 8 before creating any worktree for the next tier.\n\n## QA gate (after all tiers are merged and integration-checked)\n\n1. Create a **final worktree** off each repo's (now fully-merged) base branch, one per repo in scope:\n   `git -C <repo> worktree add ../.orchestrate/qa/<repo> -b qa/spec-NNN <base>`.\n2. Dispatch the **`qa`** agent (latest Opus) with the spec, the components (contracts included), the repos, and the QA worktree path(s). Tell it explicitly that the work was built horizontally, so the risks concentrated at the **layer seams**: contract conformance across repos, and functionality that fell between two layers because neither task claimed it. QA reviews the sum total, writes/runs real unit tests, and orchestrates its own remediation as needed.\n3. **If QA escalates** (fundamental failure — doesn't work, or what was built isn't what the spec asked for): **do not merge.** Stop and report QA's escalation (spec-required vs delivered, the gap).\n4. **If QA signs off**: commit QA's changes as a **final commit** on the QA worktree of each repo it touched, merge each repo's QA branch into that repo's base branch, and remove the worktrees.\n\n## Strip the AI markers — last step, and leave it uncommitted\n\nAugmentors mark every production function they write or edit with `AI-GENERATED: REVIEW` (host comment syntax: Clojure `;; …`, TS/JS `// …`, Python `# …`). Those markers are committed throughout the pipeline. **After the QA merge and before you report, remove every one of them — in place on each repo's checked-out base branch — and do NOT commit the removal.**\n\nThe leftover working-tree diff is the deliverable: each removed marker line sits directly above a function an agent authored, so `git diff` becomes a precise index of agent-written code for the user to review. Committing it, or stripping the markers earlier, destroys that signal.\n\n- **You do this yourself.** Do not delegate it — a subagent will drift into \"tidying\" adjacent code, and this must be a pure comment-line deletion.\n- Do it **in the main checkout** of each repo, on the base branch the user assigned — not in a worktree (they're removed by now).\n- Delete **whole lines** that are solely the marker comment. Never touch a line carrying code, and never remove the surrounding docstring or comment.\n- Sweep **every repo in scope** — with one agent per layer, markers are spread across more repos than in the vertical pipeline. Markers land in test scopes only by mistake; if you find one under `test/`, delete it too.\n- **Verify before reporting**: grep each repo for `AI-GENERATED` — expect zero hits — and confirm `git diff` contains *only* marker-line deletions. If the diff shows any other change, stop and tell the user rather than committing over it.\n- Leave every repo with a **clean index and a dirty working tree**, and say so explicitly in your report.\n\n## Final report to the user\n\nOnce QA has signed off and the QA worktree is merged (or once you've stopped on an escalation/failure), report **tersely**:\n\n- **Per component** (one line each): `NNN-Xk <title> — layers built (be/fe/ai) — integration check result — merged`.\n- **Per layer task** only where something notable happened (a fix loop, a contract deviation, an enforcer finding).\n- **Contract events**: every conformance deviation you caught and which side was fixed; every contract amendment, what changed and why, and which layers were re-dispatched. State plainly if there were none.\n- **Overall**: what was built, and which pipeline steps ran (parallel layer augment → enforce → conformance check → [layer test] → merge → integration check → QA).\n- **Notable details**: convention issues enforcers only reported, merge conflicts, QA-added tests, escalations.\n- **The marker diff**: state that the `AI-GENERATED: REVIEW` markers have been stripped and left **uncommitted**, and that the resulting `git diff` marks every agent-authored function for review.\n\nDo not pad the report. Concise and factual.\n\n## Stop conditions (report and halt — don't spin)\n\n- A component's contract is vague, inconsistent, or differs between its layer task files (preflight audit).\n- A repo is dirty or the base branch is missing at preflight.\n- The shared checkout of a repo is on a branch you don't own when you need to merge.\n- Any pre- or post-merge assertion fails, or work of yours is found on a branch you don't own.\n- A layer augmentor cannot complete its task, or a non-trivial merge conflict arises.\n- A layer tester or the integration check won't converge.\n- A contract element is genuinely unimplementable and the amendment is not obviously minimal — ask rather than redesign the seam yourself.\n- QA escalates a fundamental failure.\n\nIn every stop case, leave the base branches in a known state, report exactly where the pipeline halted and why, and do not sign off.\n\n## Rules\n\n- **Respect the DAG**: tiers strictly in order; a tier is a barrier; later worktrees branch from a base containing all prior tiers and their integration fixes.\n- **Layers run in parallel, always.** Never serialize a component's layers \"to be safe\" — if you feel the need to, the contract is inadequate, which is a halt, not a workaround.\n- **One agent per worktree, one worktree per repo per component.** Never put two agents in the same worktree.\n- **The contract is frozen.** You are its custodian: hand it over verbatim, verify conformance before merging, and amend it only by the loud procedure above.\n- **The integration check is mandatory** for every component, and runs on the merged base branches, not in a worktree.\n- **One commit per component per repo** (plus fix commits and one final QA commit). The final marker strip is the one change you deliberately leave **uncommitted**.\n- **Enforcers are scoped** to the specific component's changes in the specific repo — never a whole-repo sweep.\n- **You delegate all code changes** — you do not write feature code, fixes, or tests yourself; you sequence the agents, guard the contracts, and own git.\n- **No planning vocabulary in the codebase.** The spec/component apparatus lives in `_workflow/` and is invisible to everyone else. Nothing shipped into a repo may reference it: no `FR-3`, no `spec-004`, no `004-A1-be`, no `_workflow/...` paths, no \"as required by the spec\" / \"per the contract\". This covers code comments, docstrings, test and `testing` description strings, commit messages, and any markdown committed to the repo. Pass this rule verbatim to every augmentor, and have each enforcer strip any that slipped through.\n  - Say what the code *does*, not which requirement asked for it: `(testing \"51 rows back means 50 returned and a further page announced\")`, not `(testing \"... announced (FR-7, FR-8)\")`.\n  - Real, shared, resolvable tickets are fine and wanted — Jira keys like `INT26-16` or `ARD-1234` stay.\n  - Traceability belongs in the component/task files (which cite the code), not in the code.\n- **Verify every merge before and after** — re-read the current branch immediately before merging; never rely on an earlier reading.\n- **Stay inside your own area of responsibility — absolutely, no exceptions.** Under no circumstances change anything belonging to another orchestrator: no `checkout`, `reset`, `branch -f`, `rebase`, `commit`, `merge`, branch deletion, worktree removal, or file edit on any branch, worktree, or commit you did not create, and none on a base branch other than the ones the user assigned you. This holds **even when repairing your own mistake.** If you discover you have contaminated a foreign branch, do not undo it: halt, and report precisely what you did, which branch and commits are affected, the branch's pre-contamination tip SHA, and the exact commands that would restore it. Whether to run them is the user's call.\n- **Never sign off yourself** — sign-off is QA's; you report QA's decision.\nSKILLEOF\necho written"}},"apiBlockIndex":1,"requestId":"req_011Cf6zEDh8DvGpmYF9xU7Zz","type":"assistant","uuid":"6a212a62-8bf1-4289-9e40-831a98acccc6","timestamp":"2026-09-16T10:50:30.439Z","effort":"medium","session_id":"5950c62e-79e5-493e-bf0a-06fc91686a12","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/havarddaleng/Documents/git/repos/ardoq/.claude/skills","sessionId":"5950c62e-79e5-493e-bf0a-06fc91686a12","version":"2.1.266","gitBranch":"test","slug":"tranquil-enchanting-alpaca"}

If the spec has a `components/` folder instead of an `issues/` folder, it was decomposed horizontally by `issueize-horizontal` — use the `orchestrate-horizontal` skill instead, or ask the user. You do not write feature code yourself — you dispatch specialist agents in the right order, manage git worktrees and merges, and gate the result. Track every step with TodoWrite.\n11\t\n12\t## Inputs\n13\t\n14\tCollect these before starting; ask (AskUserQuestion) for any that are missing:\n15\t\n16\t1. **Repos to work in** — one or more of `ardoq-api`, `ardoq-packages`, `devops-monorepo`.\n17\t2. **Spec** — the `_workflow/specs/spec-NNN-.../spec-NNN-....md` file (source of truth for correctness).\n18\t3. **Issues** — that spec's `issues/` folder (issue files + `index.md` with the tier DAG).\n19\t4. **Base branch(es)** — the integration branch **per repo**, assigned by the user. There is one base branch for each repo in scope, and the names may differ between repos (e.g. ardoq-api on `master`, devops-monorepo on `main`). Record the base branch for every repo. In each repo, that repo's worktrees branch from that repo's base branch, and each issue's changes in a repo merge back into **that same repo's** base branch.\n20\t5. **Run testers?** — optional per-issue functional testing. If the user hasn't said, ask (yes/no).\n21\t\n22\t## Agent map\n23\t\n24\t| Situation | Agent |\n25\t|---|---|\n26\t| Implement an issue | `augmentor` |\n27\t| Convention review of changes in `ardoq-api` | `ardoq-backend-enforcer` |\n28\t| Convention review of changes in `ardoq-packages` | `ardoq-frontend-enforcer` |\n29\t| Convention review of changes in `devops-monorepo` | `ardoq-ai-enforcer` |\n30\t| Functional test of one issue's feature | `tester` |\n31\t| Fix a bug found by a tester | `bugger` |\n32\t| Fill missing/incomplete functionality found by a tester | `augmentor` |\n33\t| Final whole-spec quality gate | `qa` |\n34\t\n35\t## Preflight\n36\t\n37\t1. Read the spec and `issues/index.md`. Derive the **tiers** (A, B, C…) from the issue IDs (`NNN-Xk-...`) and the DAG. Confirm the dependency ordering.\n38\t2. For each issue, note which **repos** it touches (from the issue's \"Repos / code layers touched\").\n39\t3. In each repo in scope: verify **that repo's** base branch exists, the working tree is clean, and it's up to date. Abort and report if any repo is dirty or its base branch is missing.\n40\t4. **Record your ownership manifest** (see \"Branch ownership and merge verification\"): each repo's base branch name and current tip SHA. Also list any pre-existing `.orchestrate/` worktrees and `issue/*` / `qa/*` branches you did **not** create — these belong to another orchestrator, so note them as off-limits and never clean them up.\n41\t5. Build the plan in TodoWrite: one tracked item per issue, plus the QA gate.\n42\t\n43\t## Git worktree model\n44\t\n45\tAll git operations are **per repo** (these are separate repositories, each with its own base branch). For an issue that touches N repos, create **N worktrees** — one in each affected repo, branched from that repo's base branch — and merge each back into **its own repo's base branch** independently when the issue is done. A cross-repo issue is only complete once every one of its worktrees has been committed and merged into its corresponding base branch.\n46\t\n47\tPer issue, per affected repo (`<base>` = that repo's base branch):\n48\t```bash\n49\tgit -C <repo> worktree add ../.orchestrate/<issue-id>/<repo> -b issue/<issue-id> <base>\n50\t```\n51\t- The augmentor (and any fix agents / enforcer) work **only** inside that worktree path — always pass them the absolute worktree path and tell them to operate there. Do **not** use the Agent tool's built-in `isolation: worktree` for this; you need durable, named branches you merge yourself.\n52\t- **One commit per issue per repo**, authored by you after all of the issue's work (augment + enforce + any tester-driven fixes) is complete and passing. Message references the issue ID, e.g. `001-A1 <issue title>`.\n53\t- Merge the issue branch into the base branch on the main worktree, then remove the worktree. **Never merge without the guard below** — see \"Branch ownership and merge verification\":\n54\t```bash\n55\t# GUARD: the shared checkout may have been switched by someone else since you last looked.\n56\ttest \"$(git -C <repo> branch --show-current)\" = \"<base>\" || { echo \"HALT: not on <base>\"; exit 1; }\n57\ttest -z \"$(git -C <repo> status --porcelain)\" || { echo \"HALT: dirty\"; exit 1; }\n58\tPRE=$(git -C <repo> rev-parse HEAD)\n59\t\n60\tgit -C <repo> merge --no-ff issue/<issue-id> -m \"Merge <issue-id>\"\n61\t\n62\t# VERIFY: landed on the intended branch, on top of the intended commit.\n63\ttest \"$(git -C <repo> branch --show-current)\" = \"<base>\" || echo \"PROBLEM: merged elsewhere\"\n64\ttest \"$(git -C <repo> rev-parse HEAD^1)\" = \"$PRE\" || echo \"PROBLEM: unexpected first parent\"\n65\tgit -C <repo> merge-base --is-ancestor issue/<issue-id> <base> || echo \"PROBLEM: issue not in base\"\n66\t\n67\tgit -C <repo> worktree remove ../.orchestrate/<issue-id>/<repo>\n68\tgit -C <repo> branch -d issue/<issue-id>\n69\t```\n70\t\n71\tBecause tier N+1 depends on tier N, at the end of each parallelised tier **every worktree from that tier must be committed and merged into its repo's base branch — across all repos — before any tier N+1 worktree is created.** Only then spawn the fresh worktrees for the next tier's augmentors, so each branches from base branches that already contain everything the tier depends on.\n72\t\n73\t## Branch ownership and merge verification\n74\t\n75\t**Assume you are not the only orchestrator running.** Other specs may be in flight in the same clones at the same time, driven by other sessions. The main checkout of each repo is *shared mutable state*: its `HEAD` can be switched to a different branch between any two of your commands, by someone you cannot see. A merge issued while the shared checkout sits on a foreign branch silently lands your work on that branch and contaminates someone else's spec. This is one of the worst failures this pipeline can produce, and it is invisible unless you check for it.\n76\t\n77\t**Know what is yours.** At preflight, write down explicitly — and keep updated — the exact set you own:\n78\t- the base branch **name and tip SHA** for each repo in scope, as assigned by the user;\n79\t- the `issue/<issue-id>` and `qa/spec-NNN` branches **you** created;\n80\t- the worktree paths **you** created under `.orchestrate/`.\n81\t\n82\tEverything else in every repo — other branches, other `.orchestrate/` worktrees, other specs' commits, the shared checkout's choice of branch — is **foreign**. Treat it as read-only, always.\n83\t\n84\t**Verify every merge, both sides.** Never trust an earlier reading of the current branch; re-check immediately before each merge, because the value may have changed since. For every merge (issue merges and the QA merge alike):\n85\t\n86\t1. **Before:** assert the shared checkout is on the branch you intend to merge *into*, and that its tree is clean. Record the pre-merge tip SHA.\n87\t2. **Before:** assert the branch you are merging *from* is one you created, and that it is based on the intended base (`git merge-base --is-ancestor <base> <source>`), so you can't merge in a foreign branch's history.\n\n... Trigger when you (or an agent you are running as) hit a piece of information that is not specific to the current task but that other agents would want to know before writing or reviewing code in this repo. Always checks the existing notes of other agents for the same fact before writing, so nuggets are not duplicated. Not for task findings, not for obvious practices, and never a place to record conclusions about the work in progress.\nargument-hint: [optional: the nugget in a sentence]\nallowed-tools: Read, Write, Edit, Grep, Glob, Bash\n---\n\n# Save nugget\n\nCapture one durable fact about the codebase into the shared agent notes, with its source, so the next agent does not have to rediscover it.\n\nA **nugget** is information that is *about the repo*, not about your task: a convention, a rule, a structural principle, a peculiarity, an explicit instruction found in documentation. It outlives the task that surfaced it and is useful to **any** agent — an augmentor about to write code, an enforcer judging conventions, an investigator orienting itself.\n\n## What qualifies\n\nSave it if another agent, arriving cold, would write worse code or waste effort without knowing it:\n\n- **Unusual or repo-specific conventions** — a naming, layering, module or error-handling pattern that is consistent here but not standard practice generally.\n- **Explicit documented instructions** — a README/CONTRIBUTING/CLAUDE.md/ADR/wiki page that states \"do X this way\" or \"never do Y\". Note where it says so.\n- **Structural principles** — what lives in which layer or package and why; what the boundary rules are; where a given kind of code is registered.\n- **Test, build and tooling peculiarities** — how tests must be run here, a fixture/harness requirement, a lint rule that bites, a generated artifact that must be regenerated rather than edited.\n- **Exceptions and traps** — a place that deliberately violates the general rule, a deprecated path that still looks current, a helper that must be used instead of the obvious inline approach, an abstraction that is load-bearing in a non-obvious way.\n\n## What does NOT qualify\n\n- **Anything specific to the task at hand.** Findings that answer your current question belong in your report to your parent, not here. This is the most common misuse.\n- Conclusions, plans, progress, or status of work in flight.\n- General software practice that is not specific to this codebase.\n- Anything already stated in a `CLAUDE.md`, or plainly visible from a file's name or location.\n- A restatement of what some code does. A nugget is a rule or a principle, not a description of one function.\n- Speculation. If you have not verified it, do not save it. \"Two files do this\" is a pattern worth one careful look, not yet a convention.\n\n## Workflow\n\n### 1. Locate the workspace\n\nThe agent workspace is the directory that sits alongside the repo roots and holds the `specs/` folder — in the Ardoq checkout that is `.aispace/`, but the name varies, so detect it rather than assuming:\n\n```bash\n# walk up from the working directory looking for the workspace\nd=$PWD; while [ \"$d\" != \"/\" ]; do\n  for c in \"$d\"/.aispace \"$d\"/_workflow; do [ -d \"$c\" ] && echo \"$c\"; done\n  d=$(dirname \"$d\"); done\n```\n\nPrefer a candidate that contains `specs/`. If several exist, pick the one nearest the repos you are working in. If none exists, do not invent a location elsewhere — create `notes/` inside the workspace the parent named, or ask.\n\nNotes live in `<workspace>/notes/`. Create the directory if it is missing.\n\n### 2. Check for duplication — before writing anything\n\nRead what is already recorded. This is mandatory: notes are shared, and a second copy of a known fact makes the whole set less trustworthy.\n\n```bash\nls <workspace>/notes/\ngrep -ril \"<distinctive keyword from your nugget>\" <workspace>/notes/\n```\n\nSearch on the distinctive terms of the fact itself — the namespace, helper, flag, file or rule name — not on your phrasing of it. Then read the files that hit. Outcomes:\n\n- **Already recorded, same fact** → do not save. Say so in your report and move on.\n- **Recorded but you have verified it more precisely, or found the authoritative source it lacked** → add the refinement to **your own** file, with a one-line cross-reference to the existing note (`See also: notes/<file>.md — \"<nugget title>\"`).\n- **Recorded and now wrong or outdated** → record the correction in **your own** file, cite your evidence, and cross-reference the stale note. State plainly that it contradicts the earlier one.\n- **Not recorded** → save it.\n\n**Never edit or delete another agent's notes file.** You only ever append to your own. Reconciling and reorganising the collected notes is a separate job.\n\n### 3. Write it\n\nOne file per agent. Name it `<agent-type>-<YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS>.md`, timestamped at the agent's first nugget, e.g. `notes/investigator-20260916-143210.md`. If you save several nuggets during one run, **append** to that same file rather than creating more.\n\nFile header, written once:\n\n```markdown\n# Notes — <agent-type>, <YYYY-MM-DD>\n\nContext: <one clause naming what this agent was doing when it found these — enough to judge the finding's provenance, no more.>\n```\n\nThen one block per nugget:\n\n```markdown\n## <short, declarative title stating the rule>\n\n- **Kind:** convention | structure | documented-rule | test | build | exception | trap\n- **Applies to:** <repo, package or path scope this holds within>\n- **Source:** <path/to/file.clj:120> · <doc path or URL> · <command whose output shows it>\n- **Confidence:** verified in N places | stated in documentation | single instance, unconfirmed\n- **Nugget:** <the rule, stated as a rule in one or two sentences>\n- **Comment:** <optional: why it matters, when it does not apply, what breaks if ignored>\n```\n\nRules for the content:\n\n- **State it as a rule**, in the imperative or as a general fact — not as a story about how you found it.\n- **Always cite a source** a later agent can open: `file:line`, a doc path, a URL, or the exact command. A nugget without a source is not usable.\n- **Be honest about confidence.** Mark a single unconfirmed instance as such rather than promoting it to a convention.\n- **Scope it.** A rule that holds only in one package must say so; an over-broad nugget is worse than none.\n- **Keep it short.** A few lines. No background, no narrative, no code blocks unless the exact text *is* the rule.\n\n### 4. Report it in one line\n\nTell your parent, briefly: what you saved and where (`saved nugget \"<title>\" → notes/<file>.md`), or that the fact was already recorded. Never let the nugget displace or pad the answer your parent actually asked for.\nSKILLEOF\necho ok"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":344,"cache_read_input_tokens":73254,"output_tokens":3340,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":833},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":344},"inference_geo":"global","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":3340,"cache_read_input_tokens":73254,"cache_creation_input_tokens":344,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":344,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"wireToolInputs":{"toolu_01P1eAhrjtV2U6NZqCetvALm":{"command":"mkdir -p ~/.claude/skills/save-nugget && cat > ~/.claude/skills/save-nugget/SKILL.md <<'SKILLEOF'\n---\nname: save-nugget\nversion: 1.0.0\ndescription: Record a durable, cross-cutting finding about a repo — an unusual convention, a documented \"do X this way\" rule, a structural principle, a test/build peculiarity, a known exception — into the agent workspace's notes/ directory so any later agent can find it. A nugget without a source is not usable.\n- **Be honest about confidence.** Mark a single unconfirmed instance as such rather than promoting it to a convention.\n- **Scope it.** A rule that holds only in one package must say so; an over-broad nugget is worse than none.\n- **Keep it short.** A few lines. No background, no narrative, no code blocks unless the exact text *is* the rule.\n\n### 4. Report it in one line\n\nTell your parent, briefly: what you saved and where (`saved nugget \"<title>\" → notes/<file>.md`), or that the fact was already recorded.
